I do. That's... well, that's actually why I'm here. \n\nI just... I sit there afterward, looking at the empty pot or the wrappers, and... I just feel disgusted with myself. It's hard to explain. I've always been a guy who handles things, you know? Steady. In control. And then I do this and... I just feel like I'm not that man at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. It's... it's hard to stop. \n\nOnce I start, it's like... I don't know. The discipline I always had on the road, it just shuts off. I can be sitting there, telling myself, \"Magin, put the fork down.\" But... my hand just keeps going back for more. It's... yeah. I can't stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating too much happen at least once a week on average?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I mentioned earlier. Since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, it's... it's been several nights most weeks. I really can't think of a single week in the last three months where it didn't happen at least once.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been paying attention to the scale. Just... my clothes feel a bit tighter, I guess. I don't know the exact numbers.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a week would you say these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: I... like I just said, it's several nights a week. I don't keep an exact count. Just... most weeks, it's happening on multiple nights. There hasn't been a single week in the last three months where it didn't happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating too much because you can't stop yourself, or because the food just tastes really good and you're enjoying it?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's because I'm enjoying it. It's... I don't know. Once I start, it just feels like the discipline I used to have out on the road just... shuts off. I'll literally be telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. So... yeah, I guess I just can't stop.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times a month you have these episodes of eating too much?\n\nPatient: I thought I just... well, like I said, since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, it's several nights most weeks. I really don't have a monthly number for you. Just... there hasn't been a week in the last three months where it didn't happen at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered that. But... no. \n\nLike I was saying a minute ago, that discipline I used to have on the road just isn't there. I can sit there literally telling myself to stop, telling myself to put the fork down, and... I just don't. My hand just keeps going back for more. So... no, I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating too much interfered with your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose. I've been... well, I've been avoiding the phone mostly. \n\nI'm supposed to mentor some of the newer drivers, but when they call to check in, I just... let it ring. I don't really want to talk to them. I just don't want anyone hearing how far off my routine I've gotten. It's... I don't know. I just feel ashamed of it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a kind of high or euphoric feeling while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: A high? ...No. I don't really know what you mean by that. I don't get any kind of thrill out of it, if that's what you're asking. It's not like that. I'm just... eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat too much when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I guess I'm stressed sometimes. But I wouldn't say I only do it when I'm upset. It just happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Before I realize it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then maybe a couple tamales, some chips... whatever leftovers are just sitting around. It's like I’m feeding a whole work crew instead of just me.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: Well... pretty often lately. I've been off work, stuck at home with a back injury, so... I'm just around the house a lot. I guess it’s been happening several nights most weeks. It's been about three months now, and... I can't stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating too much happen at least once a week on average?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I mentioned earlier. I don't keep an exact count. Just... most weeks, it's happening on multiple nights. More than a person should. Like... some evenings I’ll heat up a bowl of pozole. I swear to myself it’s just going to be one bowl. But then... I don't know. Before I realize it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then maybe a couple tamales, some chips... whatever leftovers are just sitting around. It's like I’m feeding a whole work crew instead of just me.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: Well... pretty often lately. I've been off work, stuck at home with a back injury, so... I'm just around the house a lot. I guess it’s been happening several nights most weeks. It's been about three months now, and... I can't really think of a single week in that time where I didn't do it at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I do. That's... well, that's actually why I'm here. \n\nI just... I sit there afterward, looking at the empty pot or the wrappers, and... I just feel disgusted with myself. It's hard to explain. I've always been a guy who handles things, you know? Steady. In control. And then I do this and... I just feel like I'm not that man at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. It's... it's hard to stop. \n\nOnce I start, it's like... I don't know. The discipline I always had on the road, it just shuts off. I can be sitting there, telling myself, \"Magin, put the fork down.\" But... my hand just keeps going back for more. It's... yeah. I can't stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating too much happen at least once a week on average?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I mentioned earlier. Since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, it's... it's been several nights most weeks. I really can't think of a single week in the last three months where it didn't happen at least once.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been paying attention to the scale. Just... my clothes feel a bit tighter, I guess. I don't know the exact numbers.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a week would you say these episodes of eating too much happen?\n\nPatient: I... like I just said, it's several nights a week. I don't keep an exact count. Just... most weeks, it's happening on multiple nights. There hasn't been a single week in the last three months where it didn't happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating too much because you can't stop yourself, or because the food just tastes really good and you're enjoying it?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's because I'm enjoying it. It's... I don't know. Once I start, it just feels like the discipline I used to have out on the road just... shuts off. I'll literally be telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. So... yeah, I guess I just can't stop.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times a month you have these episodes of eating too much?\n\nPatient: I thought I just... well, like I said, since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, it's several nights most weeks. I really don't have a monthly number for you. Just... there hasn't been a week in the last three months where it didn't happen at least once.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered that. But... no. \n\nLike I was saying a minute ago, that discipline I used to have on the road just isn't there. I can sit there literally telling myself to stop, telling myself to put the fork down, and... I just don't. My hand just keeps going back for more. So... no, I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating too much interfered with your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose. I've been... well, I've been avoiding the phone mostly. \n\nI'm supposed to mentor some of the newer drivers, but when they call to check in, I just... let it ring. I don't really want to talk to them. I just don't want anyone hearing how far off my routine I've gotten. It's... I don't know. I just feel ashamed of it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a kind of high or euphoric feeling while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: A high? ...No. I don't really know what you mean by that. I don't get any kind of thrill out of it, if that's what you're asking. It's not like that. I'm just... eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat too much when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I guess I'm stressed sometimes. But I wouldn't say I only do it when I'm upset. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating too much been happening for more than 3 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose it's been about three months. Right around the time I strained my back and they took me off the road.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat too much as a way to deal with feelings of stress, anxiety, or other emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I just said, I don't really know. I'm not sitting around analyzing my feelings when it happens. It's just... I just eat. I don't think it's some deep emotional thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating too much helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it makes me feel better. I just... I just eat. I'm not really paying attention to how it makes me feel.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating too much is related to trying to cope with your feelings about being stuck at home with a back injury?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Maybe. I'm just... I'm just sitting around a lot more now. That's all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
